     2. A.   05.17.16 
2.   RECOGNITION  
A.   TSD Mission Moment—Baker Middle School Shark Tank Winners 
Tonight we honor a special group of students and teachers at Baker Middle School 
who have created innovative project ideas and marketing plans that they pitched 
to potential “would-be investors” in a Shark Tank format. 
These creative and well -thought out projects solved real world problems and 
provided solutions that could make it in the marketplace. Under the guidance of 
teachers Anne Costello, Lisa Gaglio and Colin Quinn, 8th grade students developed 
their ideas, researched feasibility and production and came up with a plan to sell 
their products to the masses. Experts from the business community as well as 
administrators and members of the Board of Education served as “Sharks,” asking 
challenging questions to the would -be inventors and requiring them to provide 
proof of concept. 
Baker Shark Tank is truly an opportunity for learning to come to life for our 
students and for them to put their skills to the test in a real world situation. 
At this time, we would like to call up teachers, Anne Costello and Lisa Gaglio to tell 
us more about this innovative project.  
Recommended Action:  Invite participants up to congratulate them.  
Students already received certificates at school. 
Students in attendance 
Anne Costello will provide names of students on Monday

2. RECOGNITION 
B.  2015-2016 Outstanding Support Staff Award    
The Troy School District has many outstanding support staff employees. This evening 
we are proud to recog nize the accomplishments of eighteen individuals who were 
nominated for the Troy School District Outstanding Support Staff Award. 
The nominees represent exemplary staff at every level and job description. They were 
nominated by their colleagues , by parents and by former and current students, all of 
whom talked about the impact these employees have on the lives of those around them. 
We are grateful for their dedication and passion for our students. 
Would you please come forward as your name is ca lled? (Distribute 
certificates/plants.)

2.       RECOGNITION 
 D.  National Teacher Appreciation Week 
Teacher Appreciation Week is observed nationally each year in May. This year, 
Teacher Appreciation Week was celebrated May 2nd – May 6th.  At tonight’s 
meeting, the Troy School District and Board of Education would like to 
recognize all of our outstanding teachers by adopting the following resolution: 
WHEREAS, throughout Troy and across the country, teachers inspire, 
nurture and open children’s minds to new ideas,  
WHEREAS, teachers promote and strengthen the principles of democratic 
equality, develop leaders and encourage critical thinking; and 
WHEREAS, Troy teachers set the bar for excellence in education and 
continually exceed the highest standards in the profession; and 
WHEREAS, the goal of Teacher Appreciation Week is to raise national and 
local awareness of teaching excellence in building a better society; now,  
THEREFORE BE IT  RESOLVED, that the Troy School District supports 
National Teacher Appreciation Week, May 2-6, 201 6, and encourages our 
schools and community to appropriately recognize the vital and changing role 
of teachers in imagining and creating a better future for society. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Read and adopt resolution. 
  Moved by:     
  Supported by:

                 2. E.    05.17.16 
2.       RECOGNITION 
E. Troy Foundation for Educational Excellence Mini-Grant Recipients 
This evening we are pleased to recognize the Troy Foundation for 
Educational Excellence and the work they do to support our schools, our 
students and our staff. The Foundation awards grants to help fund projects 
that support the curriculum in every school in the Troy S chool District. This 
year, the Foundation was able to fund  79 grants for a diverse group of 
projects to help enhance learning for students in every school in the District. 
That kind of support is invaluable. 
The Foundation is made up of volunteers who give of their time and talents 
to raise money and support innovative projects that enrich student learning. 
Again, these projects take place in every school in our District and many 
would not be possible without the resources the Foundation provides. 
At this time, I would like to introduce  Al Robinson, President of the Troy 
Foundation for Educational Excellence, who will briefly  recognize the most 
recent group of grant recipients and the many diverse ways they ar e 
enhancing the education of our Troy School District students. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION: Names of recipients/projects on screen as Mr. Robinson 
speaks.

TSD Mission Moment – Facilities 
Learning, served as the liaison for the District’s Facilities 
Study Team.   The team was formed this year in order to 
conduct  a  comprehensive  review  of  all  buildings  in  the 
comprised  entirely  of  members  of  the  community,  who 
volunteered  and  gave  countless  hours  of  their  time  and 
expertise  to  this  important  project.   Members  of  the 
Facilities  Study  Team  were  recognized  for  their 
exemplary  service.   The  Board  thanked  them  for  their 
thoughtful  and  skilled  analysis  and  added  that  the 
recommendations  they  provided  will  help  guide  the 
District in moving forward.  Special recognition was given 
to  Gary  Wood,  retired  principal  of  Costello  Elementary 
School.   Mr.  Wood  served  as  the  Community  Facilitator 
for the Facilities Study Team.  The District could not have 
undertaken  a  comprehensive  study  like  this  without  his 
leadership and direction.   The following individuals were 
asked to come up and receive a certificate of recognition 
for  their  work:    Gary  Wood,  Nicole  Balintfy,  Michael

Michigan Reading Educator 
role takes a master teacher and one who has the respect 
of the administration and staff. This year’s co‐recipient of 
Reading Association, Natalie Haezebrouck, from Leonard 
Elementary  School,  took  on  this  role  with  high 
expectations  and  vast  knowledge.  One  parent  shared 
that  she  could  watch  her  child’s  confidence  and 
enthusiasm  build  after  developing  strategies  with  Ms. 
Haezebrouck  over  the  course  of  the  year.  Natalie 
developed a Guided Reading Academy in her school. This 
academy brought teachers together to explore strategies 
that  would  be  used  in  their  classroom.  Teachers  viewed 
videos,  and  through  coaching  and  shared  experiences, 
helped  the  students  at  Leonard  reach  new  heights.  Her 
unique  strategy  was  adopted  by  the  whole  district. 
Natalie’s actions are a true reflection of the core values in 
OneTroy. The Board extended congratulations to Natalie 
Haezebrouck, who was not in attendance this evening.

Professional Learning Update 
It  was  moved  by  Mr.  Hauff  and  supported  by  Mrs. 
Fleming  that  BE  IT  THEREFORE  RESOLVED  that  the  Troy 
None. 
Mark Dziatczak, Deputy Superintendent for Teaching and 
Learning,  and  Kris  Griffor,  Assistant  Superintendent  for 
Professional Learning, which took place on April 1, 2016.  
They discussed how developing the District’s Core Values 
are  used  to  deepen  learning  for  all  students,  focus 
about  a  new  student  check  list,  ambitious  outcomes  for 
learners,  the  rubric  used  for  scoring  and  analyzing  work 
comparing  one  grade  level  to  another.   He  also  talked 
about  consistency  for  students,  and  teachers  working 
together  as  a  grade  level  team.   They  shared  that   
curriculum  is  student  driven  and  revolves  around  best 
practices.   
Mr. Hauff mentioned that this report was given in great 
detail for the Board to review at the April 12th workshop.  
Mr.  Dziatczak  thanked  the  Curriculum  and  Instruction 
Specialists  and  Jennifer  Gottlieb,  Director  of  Student 
Growth,  Equity,  and  Accountability,  who  worked 
tirelessly  in  creating  the  high  standards  for  this 
professional learning event.

Community  High  School,  reported  that  one  of  their

shared that it is the seniors last week of classes but will

School,  shared  about  the  fundraisers  held  at  Troy  High 
over the last month, including Friends for Flint, hosted by 
the Student Government.  She announced that Troy High 
had  their  annual  IASA  showcase  where  various  styles  of

5. CONSENT 
 D. CONSIDERATION:  Approve Oakland Intermediate School 
District 2016-2017 Budget 
  In accordance with Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, 
the Troy Board of Education is required to review the proposed Oakland 
Intermediate School District budget and express either its support or 
disapproval of this budget.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   Moved by  
   Supported by  
WHEREAS: 
 1. Section 624 of the Revised School Code, as amended, requires the intermediate 
school board to submit its proposed budget no later than May 1 of each year to 
the board of each constituent district for review; and 
 2. Not later than June 1 of each year, the board of each constituent district shall 
review the proposed intermediate school district budget, shall adopt a board 
resolution expressing its support for or disapproval of the proposed intermediate 
school district budget, and shall submit to the intermediate school board any 
specific objections and proposed changes the constituent district board has to 
the budget. 
NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED THAT: 
 1. The Troy Board of Education has received and reviewed the proposed 
intermediate school district budget in accordance with Section 624 of the 
Revised School Code, as amended, and, by the adoption of this resolution, 
expresses its support for the proposed intermediate school district budget.  The 
Troy School District Board of Education believes that it is the responsibility of 
120
the Intermediate School District to be responsible for its own operations, 
including adoption of its budget.  The Troy School District’s s upport of the 
Oakland Intermediate School District’s budget is for the sole purpose of 
complying with Section 624 of the Revised School Code. 
 2. The secretary of the Troy Board of Education or his/her designee shall forward 
a copy of this resolution to the intermediate school board or its superintendent 
no later than June 1, 2016. 
 3. All resolutions insofar as they conflict with this resolution be and the same are 
hereby rescinded. 
Yes:  
No:  
Resolution declared adopted. 
 Secretary, Troy Board of Education 
The undersigned duly qualified and acting Secretary of the Board of Education of the 
Troy School District, Michigan, hereby certifies that the foregoing is a true and 
complete copy of a resolution adopted by the Troy Board of Education at a regular 
meeting held on May 17, 2016, the original of which resolution is part of the Board’s 
minutes, and further certifies that notice of the meeting was given to the public under 
the Open Meetings Act, 1976 PA 267, as amended.

designate its representative and identify the Intermediate School Board 
candidate the board supports by a resolution adopted not earlier than 21 days 
prior to the date of the election, and

Good afternoon,
Two individuals have filed as candidates for the two seats open for the Oakland Schools Board of
 Education Biennial Election.
Mrs. Barbara DeMarco (6-year term)
Mr. Connie R. Williams (6-year term)
Oakland Schools board members are elected by an electoral body composed of one (1)
 person designated by the board of each constituent school district. The Revised School
 Code prescribes that each constituent school district board must designate a
 representative to this electoral body by adopting a resolution that will 1) designate the
 person to vote in the election on behalf of the local district board, 2) direct the designee
 which candidate the board supports for each position to be filled, 3) direct the designee
 to vote for those individuals, at least on the first ballot taken by the electoral body. The
 designated electors must cast their ballots in person at the election scheduled to take
 place between 5:30 – 7:30 PM on Monday, June 6, 2016 in Conference Room B of the
 Oakland Schools building located at 2111 Pontiac Lake Road in Waterford.  The revised
 school code prohibits the acceptance of absentee ballots.
According to the revised school code, districts shall consider the resolution at no less than
 one (1) meeting before adopting the resolution. Districts have interpreted this language
 differently and historically have used one of the following three options to consider and
 adopt their resolutions:
Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the
 resolution as an action item during another open meeting, with each meeting
 occurring on a separate date
Consider the resolution as a discussion item during an open meeting and adopt the
 resolution as an action item during a second open meeting immediately following
 the first open meeting
Consider the resolution as a discussion item and adopt the resolution as an action
 item at the same open meeting (Local school districts selecting this option should
 seek independent legal counsel confirming a singular open meeting to consider and
 adopt a resolution is compliant with the revised school code).
127
According to the revised school code, districts must adopt their resolutions no earlier than
 twenty-one (21) days prior to the election (no earlier than May 16, 2016).
After your board has adopted its resolution as prescribed by the revised school
 code, please fax (248.209.2206) a copy of the resolution to the Oakland Schools’
 Superintendent’s office by the end of the business day on Friday, June 3, 2016. This
 will enable us to compile a list of district designees prior to the election date.
Attached is a copy of the Revised School Code, a sample resolution template to designate
 your district’s representative and selection of candidates, and an election timeline.
An official notice of this election will be sent to your board of education secretary via
 registered mail.
If you have any questions regarding board elections or the ISD board election process
 please contact Sherry McMillan at 248.209.2062 or by email at
Note: As the candidates are the incumbents and running un-opposed, a “Meet the
 Candidates” forum will not be scheduled.
Thank You,
Kathy Boike
Executive Assistant
Office of the Superintendent & Board of Education
Oakland Schools
2111 Pontiac Lake Road
Waterford, MI  48328
